OnCo · weekly issue

OnCo weekly, 2026-11-17

1534 changelog entries, 5 regulatory events, 2 upcoming dates, 1126 corrections. Generated from the corpus; every item links to its page and, through it, to the primary source. Not medical advice.

What changed this week

New pages, new data and fixes, from the changelog.

  • 17 November 2026: Second accuracy pass on the original 39 country pages, among them the United States, China, India, Japan, Germany, Brazil and the United Kingdom: about 660 claims corrected after each was read against its source again
  • 17 November 2026: Second accuracy pass on live country pages, last batch for waves 5 to 12: corrections to Austria, Serbia, Denmark, Finland, Norway, Ireland, Hungary, Belarus, Bulgaria and North Korea, each claim read against its source again

  • 17 November 2026: Second accuracy pass on live country pages: corrections to 53 pages after every claim was read against its source again, including what the IAEA radiotherapy directory lists for 36 countries, WHO's HPV vaccination data where pages had said there was none, and sources pages had called unreadable that do answer
  • 17 November 2026: The first weekly gap fill read from FDA sources lands, each fact checked against the FDA list and device records: the Guardant360 Liquid CDx blood test approved on 19 May 2026 with its seven drug pairings, and four companion tests linked to the biomarkers they read
  • 17 November 2026: More of the graph is joined up: 1,251 confirmed links between people and the trials whose papers they wrote, matched by ORCID or full name and affiliation; who pays for 21 tests in the US and UK, from Medicare national coverage decisions and NICE guidance; fifty warning-card lines now name the protocol that manages them; and eight wrong side-effect links are removed
  • 17 November 2026: Twenty-two more care protocols close the gaps in the census, among them stoma care, drain care at home, transplant conditioning, veno-occlusive disease, lung biopsy, intrathecal chemotherapy in children, end-of-life care for children, advance care planning and stopping smoking after a diagnosis; 212 protocols in all, with a table of what is covered and what is still open

Regulatory events, past 7 days

Dated filings, approvals, letters and label changes recorded on product pages.

  • 2026-11-16 · Giredestrant · approval (US): FDA approved giredestrant (Wilserdly, Genentech, Inc.), an oral estrogen receptor antagonist, in combination with everolimus for the treatment of adults with estrogen receptor (ER)-positive, human epidermal growth factor receptor 2 (HER2)-negative, estrogen receptor 1 ( ESR1) -mutated locally advanced or metastatic breast cancer, as detected by an FDA-authorized test, whose disease has recurred during or after completion of endocrine-based adjuvant therapy, or has progressed on at least one endocrine-based regimen in the metastatic setting. source
  • 2026-11-15 · Ivonescimab · crl (US): Complete response letter, announced by Summit on 15 November 2026 (the letter's own date is not stated): "The CRL indicates that the BLA cannot be approved in its present form, as the FDA determined that substantial evidence of effectiveness is not established in the absence of a statistically significant overall survival (OS) improvement." source
  • 2026-11-14 · Ivonescimab · pdufa (US): PDUFA target date source
  • 2026-11-13 · Durvalumab · approval (US): FDA approved durvalumab (Imfinzi, AstraZeneca) in combination with enfortumab vedotin-ejfv as neoadjuvant treatment, followed by single agent durvalumab as adjuvant treatment after radical cystectomy, for the treatment of adult patients with muscle invasive bladder cancer (MIBC) who are ineligible for or who have refused cisplatin based chemotherapy. source
  • 2026-11-10 · Pimicotinib · approval (US): FDA approved pimicotinib (Pimjetmi, EMD Serono, Inc.), a kinase inhibitor, for adults with symptomatic tenosynovial giant cell tumor (TGCT) for which surgical resection may cause worsening functional limitation or severe morbidity. The notice adds: "Pimicotinib received fast track and breakthrough therapy designation." source

(Second accuracy pass; WHO immunisation data queried with the broad HPV filter (API JSON), Ministry of Health page of 10 February 2021 (Internet Archive capture, raw HTML)) · The card now says the rows run from 2010 with no values before 2022, keeps WHO's estimates (first dose 70%, 96%, 99%, 95% for 2022 to 2025), adds the country's own administrative reports (95.78% in 2023, 103.81% in 2024, 149.59% in 2025), and says "a vaccination campaign in two health districts". The link uses the broad filter.
  • 2026-11-16 · Burkina Faso country page: The screening card named the organiser of the 2023 breast screening campaign as the "Coalition Burkinabè Contre le Cancer" and said the campaign would assess capacity for "breast ultrasound and mammography"; the Ministry's page says "coalition burkinabè de lutte contre le cancer (COBUCAN)" and "écho-mammographies". (Second accuracy pass; Ministry of Health page of 24 April 2023 (Internet Archive capture, raw HTML)) · Reworded to "the Burkinabè coalition against cancer (COBUCAN)" and "breast imaging (écho-mammographies in the Ministry's report)".
